# Fabricated: fixed findings

Every defect found and fixed, by ID: where it was, how it failed and what triggered it, why the tests missed it, and
the fix with how it was verified. Open findings are in [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md#findings); a finding moves here, in
the same change, once its fix is verified. Code, commits and the other documents cite findings by ID.

Severity: **P0** crash or data loss on a normal launch · **P1** a supported feature silently doesn't work ·
**P2** wrong behaviour or a leak under realistic conditions · **P3** diagnostics, hygiene, unlikely conditions.

| ID | Sev | Location | Failure mode and trigger | Why tests missed it | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CORPUS-94 | P0 on Forge 47 servers with Supplementaries (the corpus server stopped in its data reload); on Forge 47 for any Fabric mod reading `MobBucketItem`'s entity type through an accessor | Forge 47's `MobBucketItem` (its `entityTypeSupplier`, where vanilla has the field `type`, `f_151134_`); Supplementaries' `MobBucketItemAccessor`, Inventory Profiles Next's `IMixinEntityBucketItem`; `MixinRepairs.retypedFieldBridge`, `forge-legacy`'s `MobBucketItemBridgeMixin` (new) | With CORPUS-90 fixed, the Forge 47 corpus server stopped in its first data reload (extended run 41712171106): `NoSuchMethodError: MobBucketItemAccessor.getType()` from Supplementaries' dispenser behaviours, which Moonlight registers after a reload. Forge 47 replaced vanilla's `type` field with a supplier of the entity type, so pre-flight left the accessor out ("@Accessor target field f_151134_ does not exist", as for Inventory Profiles Next's) and the mod's call of it found nothing. | CORPUS-37's repair took only a field the platform gave another type, and no test had an accessor of a field the platform removed. | Pre-flight points an accessor of a field the platform replaced at the bridge Fabricated's in-game mod adds for it, as for a retyped one (`MixinRepairs.retypedFieldBridge`; `MixinPreflightTest.anAccessorOfAFieldThePlatformReplacedInvokesFabricatedsBridge`: with the bridge the accessor invokes it, without it pre-flight reports the field missing as before). On Forge 47 the in-game mod gives `MobBucketItem` a `fabricated$get$f_151134_` returning the bucket's entity type (Forge's `getFishType`). Verified on Forge 47 (#271): the test mod's accessor read a pufferfish bucket's type in PR run 41722621312 and extended run 41723295489, and in the extended run the corpus server got through its data reloads and started. |
| CORPUS-91 | P0 on Forge 47 with Create (the corpus server and client: its main entrypoint threw), and the mods needing it | Fabric API 1.20.1's `OxidizableMixin` (an `@Inject` in an interface mixin into `WeatheringCopper`'s `NEXT_BY_BLOCK` supplier, `method_34740`), `OxidizableBlocksRegistry.registerOxidizableBlockPair`; `InterfaceInjects` (new, core), the Forge branches' `MixinExtensions` | Create's main entrypoint threw `UnsupportedOperationException` from `ImmutableMap.put` (extended run 41703581030): `CopperRegistries.inject` registers Create's copper blocks through Fabric API's `OxidizableBlocksRegistry`, which puts them in vanilla's oxidation map. Upstream's mixin makes that map a `HashBiMap` copy of vanilla's immutable one; it is an injector in an interface mixin, which Forge 47's Mixin 0.8.5 doesn't apply, so pre-flight left it out (INTERFACE-1) and the map stayed immutable. | Pre-flight left out every injector in an interface mixin on Forge 47, as Mixin 0.8.5 applies none, and no test used a Fabric API registry whose upstream hook is one. | Where Mixin applies no injector in an interface mixin, translation makes an `@Inject` at `HEAD`, `RETURN` or `TAIL` a public synthetic method Mixin merges into its target, marked with where it injects, and the Forge branches' Mixin extension makes it private again and calls it there as Mixin's `CallbackInjector` would (`InterfaceInjectsTest`, `PipelineTest.anInterfaceMixinsInjectAtHeadIsKeptForFabricatedToApplyWhereMixinDoesnt`). Other injectors in interface mixins are left out as before. Verified on Forge 47 (#271): the `forge-legacy` test mod's pair, registered through `OxidizableBlocksRegistry`, is in vanilla's oxidation map (PR run 41722621312), and Create's main entrypoint ran on the corpus server and client (extended run 41723295489). The fix also made AppleSkin's tooltip mixin, which extends its target, an accessor mixin to Mixin again: CORPUS-96. |
| CORPUS-79 | P0 on Forge 61 and 64 clients with ResourcefulLib (the corpus clients); on Forge 61 to 66 for a local Mixin finds after such frames | ResourcefulLib's `ScreenEffectRendererMixin`, Forge 61's and 64's `ScreenEffectRenderer.renderScreenEffect`; Mixin 0.8.7's `ClassInfo.FrameData` and `Locals.getLocalsAt`, Forge's ModLauncher (`ClassTransformer`); `RecordedFrames` (new, core), the Forge branches' `RecordedFrameRepair` | The 26.1.2 corpus client crashed initializing the game (extended run 41594474962), and the 1.21.11 one too (extended run 41632339327): "Critical injection failure" of ResourcefulLib's `rlib_renderScreenEffect`, an `@Inject` at `INVOKE LocalPlayer.isEyeInFluid(TagKey)` (`shift = BEFORE`) taking an implicit `@Local PoseStack`, which pre-flight let through. Mixin's own cause (extended run 41658054089) was "Found 0 candidate variables but exactly 1 is required": Mixin 0.8.7's `Locals.getLocalsAt` gave no `PoseStack` there, though the jar's method has one in scope by its local variable table and frames. Read in Mixin 0.8.7's source: `getLocalsAt` takes each frame's change in size from the frames Mixin recorded for the class (`ClassInfo.FrameData`), recorded from the class read without expanding its frames (unless its `CLASSREADER_EXPAND_FRAMES` option is set), and it records an appending or chopping frame's size as at least the method's argument slots (`computeFrameSize`), not the locals appended or chopped. `renderScreenEffect` has four argument slots, appends three locals and chops one: Mixin counts 4 + 4, then 8 - 4, four locals where six are live, and drops the `PoseStack`. Forge's ModLauncher fork (10.2, which Forge 61 to 66 run) hands Mixin the class with its frames compressed, so nothing corrects the count. Mixin 0.8.5 (Forge 47) records the frames expanded, and Fabric's Mixin records each frame's own size: the mod works on Fabric. On Forge 65 and 66, ResourcefulLib 5 and 6 hook `submit` instead. | No test applied a mixin taking a local in a method whose frames append fewer locals than its argument slots and then chop. The first fix (#266's `RecordedFrames.droppingChops`, #267 and #268) took the frames Mixin walks to be expanded, as McModLauncher's ModLauncher reads them; no test walked compressed frames, and the Forge test mod's probe failed on all four Forge 61 to 66 versions (#268's PR run 41703553076 and extended run 41703586244). | Before mixins apply to a class a Fabric mod's mixin targets, Fabricated's Mixin extension on Forge finds the methods whose locals Mixin would count wrong at some frame (`RecordedFrames.counted`: Mixin's count from the frames it recorded, against its count from the same frames expanded) and records their frames again from the method with its frames expanded (`RecordedFrames.withExpandedFrames`, as ASM expands them, through Mixin's own `ClassInfo.Method`). Methods Mixin counts right, and classes no Fabric mod's mixin targets, keep its frames. `RecordedFramesTest` covers Forge's shape (8 then 4 locals counted where there are 7 then 6, and right once recorded expanded, whether the frames walked are compressed or expanded), vanilla's shape, a chopped long, a method Mixin counts right, and the expanded frames of an instance method, a constructor with uninitialized values and a static method with every kind of argument. Verified on Forge 61 to 66 (#268): the Forge test mod's probe passed on every version in PR run 41718380187, and in extended run 41718376778 the Forge 61 corpus client reached its world and the Forge 64 one got past ResourcefulLib's hook (it stops later, on CORPUS-95). Forge 47's Mixin needs no repair. |
| CORPUS-90 | P0 on Forge 47 servers with Porting Lib Loot (Create's; the corpus server); on Forge, and NeoForge before 26.3, for any Fabric mod naming a loot table as it is built | Fabricated's loot table hooks: `forge-legacy`'s `LootDataManagerMixin`, NeoForge's and Forge 61 to 65's `LootBridge`, Forge 66's `ForgeHooksLootTableNameMixin` (new); Porting Lib Loot's `LootTableMixin` and its builder mixin | With CORPUS-86 fixed, the Forge 47 corpus server stopped loading its data packs (extended runs 41698783756 and 41703581030), and the client's integrated server too: "Attempted to rename loot table from 'minecraft:blocks/orange_wool' to 'minecraft:blocks/orange_wool': this is not supported", from Fabricated's hook on the loot data manager. Porting Lib Loot names every table on Fabric as Forge does (read in its 1.20.1 and 1.21.1 sources): its MODIFY listener hands the builder the table's id, and its builder mixin names the table it builds. The hook then named the rebuilt table again, as Forge names a table it loads, and Forge refuses a second name, even the same one; so do NeoForge 21.1 to 26.2 and Forge 61 to 65 (read in their patches; NeoForge 26.3 accepts the same name). Forge 66 names each table itself, in `ForgeHooks.onJsonDataParsed`, after Fabricated's hook has handed it to Fabric's events (read in Forge 66's source). | No test mod named a table as it was built, and the Forge 47 corpus server stopped at Moonlight's data packs (CORPUS-86) before it reached the loot tables. | The hooks name a rebuilt table unless it already has that name; on Forge 66 the in-game mod's loot config wraps Forge's naming call to leave a table that already has its name. The test mods name dirt's table from MODIFY and from a builder mixin calling the table's name setter, and their loot checks require it to load with that name, where nothing else names it (Porting Lib Loot, or another mod's loot module). Verified on Forge 47 (#269, extended run 41712171106: the corpus server loaded its loot tables, Porting Lib Loot naming every one), NeoForge (#270's PR run: the test mods' loot checks) and Forge 61 to 66 (#268's PR run 41718380187: the test mod's loot check on every version). |
| CORPUS-86 | P0 on Forge 47 servers with Moonlight (the corpus server); on Forge 47 for any Fabric mod's override of a method Intermediary leaves under its official name, where the descriptor names no Minecraft class | `IntermediaryRemapper.plainMethod` and `ancestors`, `Supertypes` (new), `MappingTable.ownPlainMethod` and `isPlainMethod` (new), `JarRemapper`, `ClassIndex.supertypes` (new); Moonlight's `CaveFilter.Type` | The Forge 47 corpus server stopped loading data packs (extended run 41681558702): "AbstractMethodError: Receiver class net.mehvahdjukaar.moonlight.core.misc.CaveFilter$Type does not define or inherit an implementation of the resolved method 'abstract com.mojang.serialization.Codec m_191869_()' of interface …PlacementModifierType". Intermediary leaves `PlacementModifierType.codec()` under its official name, and SRG names it `m_191869_`. PLAIN-1 renames such a method by its owner, or by its name and descriptor where the descriptor names a Minecraft class. Moonlight's `CaveFilter.Type`, implementing the interface, declares `codec()` returning DataFixerUpper's `Codec`, so its override kept `codec` and the interface's method had no implementation. | PLAIN-1's tests renamed plain methods in the class declaring them and in calls naming that class or naming a Minecraft class in the descriptor; none overrode one in a mod's class. | A plain method a mod's class declares or a call names takes the name its nearest supertype declaring it gives it, found through the jar's own classes, then the game's (Forge 47's SRG table only; NeoForge's and Forge 61 to 66's have no plain methods). `RemapTest.anOverrideOfAMethodOnlySrgRenamesTakesItsNameFromTheTypeItInherits` covers an interface, a superclass of the mod's, one of the game's, another plain method, and a method of the mod's own, which keeps its name. Limit: a class extending another Fabric mod's class isn't followed into that mod. Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41703581030, after #267): the corpus server loaded Moonlight's data packs, with no `AbstractMethodError`, and went on to its loot tables, where it stopped on CORPUS-90. |
| CORPUS-84 | P0 on Forge 47 clients with MoreCulling (the corpus client); on every platform for a mixin's `@Shadow` of a method its target only inherits | `MixinRepairs.inheritedShadowRepair` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkMembersOf`; MoreCulling's `MultipartBakedModel_cacheMixin`, Forge 47's `MultiPartBakedModel` | The corpus client crashed in its loading overlay, baking the models (extended run 41658060068): Mixin rejected MoreCulling's `MultipartBakedModel_cacheMixin` ("@Shadow method m_213637_ ... was not located in the target class") as FerriteCore's multipart deduplication loaded `MultiPartBakedModel`. The mixin shadows `getQuads(BlockState, Direction, RandomSource)`. Forge 47 makes the model implement `IDynamicBakedModel` and moves that method into the interface's default, which calls the model's own `getQuads` with no model data and every render type, as vanilla's does. Mixin finds a shadowed method only among the target's own (`TargetClassContext.findMethod`, read in Fabric's Mixin and SpongePowered's); pre-flight looked it up through the supertypes, found the default, and let it by. | Every shadowed method in the tests was the target's own or missing. | Pre-flight looks for a shadowed method among the target's own, and fails one the target only inherits (or that a supertype it can't read may have). The repairs drop the shadow of an instance method the target inherits, where every use of it in the mixin is virtual, so the mixin's calls of it, which Mixin points at the target, reach the inherited method (`MixinPreflightTest.aShadowOfAMethodTheTargetOnlyInheritsIsDroppedForTheInheritedOne` fails without it). Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41703581030, after #267): the corpus client baked its models, with no shadow error, and went on to load its world's data packs. |
| CORPUS-80 | P1 on Forge 47 to 66 with Farmer's Delight (refabricated), and the mods needing it (Supplementaries and Moonlight on Forge 47) | Forge 61 to 66: `EnumValuesMixins.holderClass`, `withHeldConstants` and `initReturns` (new), `ModTranslator.valuesMixin` (enum mixins into `RecipeBookType` and `SearchRecipeBookCategory`); Forge 47: `EarlyRisers` and `ModTranslator.earlyRisers` (new), `ModTranslator.processMixins` (Fabric ASM's enum additions) | On Forge 61 to 66 pre-flight disables Farmer's Delight (extended run 41632339327): its `RecipeBookTypeMixin` and `SearchRecipeBookCategoryMixin` are enum mixins, which Forge's Mixin 0.8.7 doesn't apply, and ENUM-7 didn't turn them into ordinary mixins because the mod's own classes read the constants as fields of the enums (`FDRecipeBookTypes.COOKING = RecipeBookType.FARMERSDELIGHT_COOKING`, and its recipe book screen's search category), which no mixin can add. Had it, the game would have stopped as `RecipeBookType` loaded: Forge makes that enum extensible, its `create` factory runs `init` on each constant it makes, and Forge 61 to 66's `init` calls `RecipeBookSettings.register`, which throws "This is not implemented yet" (Forge's 1.21.10 to 26.3 patches). On Forge 47 it adds its recipe book types with Fabric ASM (`ClassTinkerers.enumBuilder` in an early riser, nested Fabric ASM 2.3), which Fabricated doesn't run: `FarmersDelight`'s class initializer failed, and Supplementaries and Moonlight, which use its classes, threw `NoClassDefFoundError` (extended runs 41590139262 and 41632355968; the evidence step printed the calls). | Forge 61 to 66: ENUM-7's test disabled a mod reading a constant as a field, by design, and no test had an extensible enum whose `init` throws. Forge 47: no test had a mod using Fabric ASM, whose plugin can't run on Forge (it adds classes to Fabric's class loader; Mixin drops its config). Then ENUM-7's test took Forge 47's `RecipeBookType.init` as one to run, since it returns (`EnumValuesMixinsTest`). | Forge 61 to 66: the mod's reads of the constants as fields of the enum, and any in the code moved into the enum, read them from a holder class generated next to the mixins. Its fields are set from the enum's values by name; a constant missing there (its mixin didn't apply) fails the holder with the enum, the constant and the mixin named. Into an extensible enum whose `init` can't return, the mixin cancels `init` for the mod's constants only, which Fabric gives no such hook; other constants keep it (`PipelineTest.anEnumMixinsConstantsTheModReadsAsFieldsOfTheEnumComeFromAHolderWhereMixinAppliesNoEnumMixins`, and two `EnumValuesMixinsTest` tests that run the code). Limit: another mod reading these constants as fields (an addon, through the class tweaker's `transitive-extend-enum`) still finds none. Forge 47: Fabric ASM's config is left out. Translation reads the early risers' `enumBuilder` calls without running them and writes the constants as enum mixins of its own, which ENUM-7 turns into ordinary mixins calling Forge's `create` factories. A supplier's arguments come from a helper next to it, which runs it as the enum is initialized (`EarlyRisersTest`, `PipelineTest.fabricAsmsEarlyRisersEnumConstantsComeFromEnumMixinsOfFabricatedsOwn`). Extended run 41675005964 (after #263's sync) made the constants, and Farmer's Delight's entrypoint then failed with a `NullPointerException`: Forge's `create` runs `init` on each constant it makes, and Forge 47's `RecipeBookType.init` registers the type's tags with `RecipeBookSettings`. That class's initializer then ran while `RecipeBookType` was still initializing from `FarmersDelight`'s (`RECIPE_TYPE_COOKING = RecipeBookType.valueOf(...)`), and the mod's `RecipeBookSettingsMixin` added its type's tags there from that field, still null. The client stopped next, as Forge's `RecipeBookManager.init` initialized `RecipeBookCategories`: the meals category's supplier made an item stack of an item the failed entrypoint never registered ("No delegate exists for value air"). Fix: Forge's `init` is skipped for the mod's constants also where it reaches beyond the enum (`EnumValuesMixins.initKeepsToEnum`, new). Fabric gives them no such hook, and the mod adds its tags itself; an `init` keeping to the enum (Forge 47's `MobCategory`'s, filling the map its codec reads) still runs. Verified on Forge 61 to 66 in extended run 41682699918, where Farmer's Delight loaded and ran its entrypoints on the Forge 61, 64, 65 and 66 corpus servers and clients, and every server started; on Forge 47 in extended run 41703581030, after #267, with CORPUS-37's fix: Farmer's Delight's entrypoints ran on the corpus server and client, Supplementaries and Moonlight loaded on both, and the client got past `RecipeBookManager.init`. |
| CORPUS-77 | P2 on Forge 47 clients with owo-lib (the corpus client); on Forge for any mod asking every mod for its root | owo's client entrypoint (its `ModDataLoader`), Fabricated's mod container for MixinExtras (`Pipeline.builtinFiles`) | owo's client initializer threw "Mod mixinextras has no root path" (extended run 41590139262): the container Fabricated lists for the MixinExtras the in-game mod nests has no root path on Forge 47. owo's data loader resolves each mod's `data` folder in its root, asking every mod in `getAllMods()` for `getRootPath()`. The container's files were those of NeoForge's mod, which ships MixinExtras; on Forge there is no such mod, so it had none. | `PipelineTest`'s check of the built-in mods' roots left MixinExtras out. | On Forge, MixinExtras' files are the in-game mod's, which nests it (`PipelineTest.builtInModsHaveTheRootOfWhatProvidesThem` checks it with and without NeoForge). Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41703581030, after #267): owo's client entrypoint ran on the corpus client. |
| CORPUS-37 | P2 on Forge 47 with Porting Lib (Create Fabric, Farmer's Delight Refabricated; the corpus server and client) | Porting Lib's accessors module (Forge 47 corpus: Create Fabric, Farmer's Delight Refabricated); `MixinRepairs.retypedFieldBridge` (new), `RestoredFields`; `forge-legacy`'s `PotionBrewingMixBridgeMixin` and `ParticleEngineProvidersBridgeMixin` (new) | Pre-flight disables it: accessors of fields Forge 47 retyped (`MinecraftServer.f_129740_`, `PotionBrewing$Mix.f_43532_` and `f_43534_`, and on the client `ParticleEngine.f_107293_`, PART-1's map), and with it the modules and mods needing it (LINK-2 for the base module's texture interface). In extended run 41681558702 pre-flight failed the accessors of `PotionBrewing$Mix.f_43532_` and `f_43534_` (vanilla's potion or item; Forge 47's `Holder.Reference`), and on the client `ParticleEngine.f_107293_` (vanilla's `Int2ObjectMap` by raw id; Forge 47's map by id). Farmer's Delight's entrypoint then threw `ClassNotFoundException` for the extensions module's `IShearable` (CORPUS-80). | Pre-flight's tests failed an accessor of a retyped field, by design, and nothing bridged one. | The in-game mod adds a getter to the field's class for each retyped field a Fabric mod reads, `fabricated$get$` and the field's runtime name, giving vanilla's type: the holder's value, and a view by raw id of Forge's map. An `@Accessor` of the field becomes an `@Invoker` of that bridge, keeping its own name and descriptor, so callers (Create calls Porting Lib's) are served as before (`PipelineTest.aFieldThePlatformRetypedIsReachedThroughFabricatedsBridge`, `MixinPreflightTest.anAccessorOfAFieldThePlatformRetypedInvokesFabricatedsBridge`; `forge-legacy`'s test mod reads a mix's potions and the providers through such accessors). The fields are final, so no setter is bridged. Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41703581030, after #267): pre-flight let Porting Lib's accessors through, and its modules loaded on the corpus server and client, but for its GUI utilities on the client (CORPUS-93); Farmer's Delight's entrypoint found the extensions module's `IShearable`. |
| CORPUS-36 | P2 on Forge 61 to 66 with Farmer's Delight Refabricated (the corpus) | Farmer's Delight Refabricated (Forge 61 to 66 corpus) | Disabled: it "adds enum constants with Mixin's enum extensions, which need Mixin 0.17.1 or later; this Forge runs Mixin 0.8.7". Forge reads no enum extension file to translate them into (ENUM-1 is NeoForge's). Read (extended run 41632339327): ENUM-7 left its enum mixins as they were, as its classes read their constants as fields of the enums. | ENUM-7's test disabled a mod reading a constant as a field, by design (CORPUS-80). | Fixed by CORPUS-80, verified in extended run 41682699918: Farmer's Delight loaded on the Forge 61 to 66 corpus servers and clients. |
| CORPUS-85 | P0 on Forge 61 to 66 clients with a Fabric mod's recipes holding Fabric API's custom ingredients (the Forge 65 and 66 corpus clients) | Forge's `Ingredient.CONTENTS_STREAM_CODEC` and `OPTIONAL_CONTENTS_STREAM_CODEC`; `FabricIngredientCodec` (new), `OptionalIngredientCodec`, `IngredientNetworkCodecMixin` (renamed from `IngredientOptionalCodecMixin`; `forge`) | The Forge 65 and 66 corpus clients waited for their player until the run's timeout (extended run 41675011378): Fabric API's recipe sync failed to encode ("Modders must implement Ingredient.codec in their custom Ingredients"). Forge 61 to 66's patch writes every ingredient through its `serializer()`, which throws for a subclass that doesn't override it; a Fabric mod's subclass never does, and Fabric API's custom ingredients (`CustomIngredientImpl`) are one. | `ForgeIngredientCodecTest` round-tripped only vanilla's ingredients. The Forge 65 corpus client that verified CORPUS-83 (extended run 41648273936) joined its world; which synced recipe holds the custom ingredient, and so why it didn't fail then, isn't known. | A Fabric mod's subclass leaving `serializer()` as `Ingredient` has it is written as vanilla writes it, after a marker of its own (-2, which neither Forge's reader, Fabric API's nor vanilla's holder set uses), through the codec Forge keeps for vanilla's ingredients, which Fabric API's mixin wraps with its custom form. In a recipe slot, Forge's custom ingredients are read by Forge's codec, as Fabric API's wrapper took their -1 for its own. Every other ingredient is written as before, and a Forge mod's subclass without `serializer()` still gets Forge's error. The test mod round-trips its own subclass alone, in a slot and inside Forge's compound ingredient, and one of Fabric API's custom ingredients alone and in a slot (`ci/forge/smoke-server.sh` requires both lines). Verified in extended run 41682699918: the Forge 65 and 66 corpus clients joined their worlds, and the round trips passed on every Forge version's must-pass servers (#261). |
| CORPUS-72 | P1 on Forge 47 with Modern Industrialization (the corpus) | Magna (its nested tool library), Modern Industrialization's `MIStartup`; `CallRepairs.movedCallRepairs`, `vanillasPlace` and `movedInto` (new) | Modern Industrialization's main entrypoint threw `ClassNotFoundException: dev.draylar.magna.api.MagnaTool` (extended runs 41590139262 and 41675005964): pre-flight disabled Magna, whose classes Modern Industrialization's items extend. Magna's hammers break their area from an `@Inject` at `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock`'s `playerWillDestroy` call, which cancels the break. Forge 47's patch moved that call, with vanilla's `removeBlock` and `destroy`, into a `removeBlock` of its own, called in the creative and the survival branch, which reaches `playerWillDestroy` through its extensions' `onDestroyedByPlayer`. | VANILLA-1's repairs moved a point to what the patch put in the call's place, and HOOK-3's to a call of the platform's code leading to it; none moved one to a place the patch left empty, and HOOK-3 didn't follow a method the patch added to the game's class. | An `@Inject` at a call the patch moved out of the method, into methods the patch added or the platform's code (followed three methods deep), moves to vanilla's place: before the first call after it that both versions make once, when the patched code between the last such call before it and that one does what vanilla's did, apart from loading values. At the patch's `removeBlock` calls it would run after the survival branch damages the tool, and once more for each block Magna checks through `destroyBlock`. Only an `@Inject` capturing no locals moves (`MixinPreflightTest.anInjectionAtACallThePatchMovedIntoAMethodOfItsOwnStaysAtVanillasPlace`, which compiles both versions with javac). Verified in extended run 41681558702: Magna and Modern Industrialization loaded on the Forge 47 corpus server and client, with no entrypoint failure (#264, synced in #265). Why Modern Industrialization loaded without Magna is CORPUS-89 (open). |
| CORPUS-55 | P0 on Forge 47 with Porting Lib (the corpus client) | Porting Lib Entity's `GuiMixin`, `MixinPreflight.indexedLocalOfOtherType`, `MixinPreflight.slotNeverHolds`, `LocalRepairs.indexedLocalRepairs` (new) | The Forge 47 corpus client stopped as it loaded `Gui` (extended run 41563919281, the console CI now prints): Mixin's `InjectionError` for `porting_lib_entity.mixins.json:client.GuiMixin`, handler `shouldRenderGuiIcon`, a `@WrapWithCondition` of `List.add` in `Gui.renderEffects` (`m_280523_`), caused by "SugarApplicationException: Failed to validate sugar @Local(index = 8) MobEffectInstance ... Unable to find matching local!". The handler takes the loop's effect, draw position and alpha by vanilla's slots (`@Local(index = 8) MobEffectInstance`, `index = 10` and `11` ints, `index = 12` float; read in its 1.20.1 source); Forge 47's `Gui`, recompiled from decompiled source, keeps other locals there, and Mixin's discriminator takes a slot only where its local has the handler's type exactly. Pre-flight judged `@Local` by name or type, never by index. The `IllegalStateException: Couldn't find method in TransformingClassLoader` this row first named is Fabric ASM's (`com.chocohead.mm.Plugin.fishAddURL`): Mixin catches it and drops that config ("Failed to select mixin config"), and the game goes on. | Pre-flight judged `@Local` by name or type, never by index, and no test took a local by a slot another build keeps another local in. | Fix: an `@Local(index = N)` whose slot never holds a value of its type in this build (no table entry of the type; every value stored there of another kind, or of a class none of whose superclasses is it) takes the slot the instructions storing vanilla's slot store to here (vanilla's method aligned with this build's, VANILLA-1), or else the only local of the type in scope where the injector applies; where neither tells, the injector is judged failed as its `require` says (`MixinPreflightTest.aLocalTakenByItsIndexMovesToTheSlotThisBuildKeepsItInWhereItsSlotNeverHoldsOne`). Not judged: a slot that holds another local of the type here, which MixinExtras then takes. Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41658060068, after the sync #259): the corpus client made its `Gui` and reached its loading overlay without Porting Lib's injection failure, with Porting Lib Entity (in Create's jar) neither disabled nor loaded without some of its mixins; it stops later, baking models, on CORPUS-84. |

The same on NeoForge 21.1: the 1.21.1 corpus client crashed in its world (exit status 255, extended run 41512581949) on Puzzles Lib's `ScreenEffectRendererFabricMixin`, a `@ModifyVariable` at the `STORE` of a `BlockState` in `ScreenEffectRenderer.renderScreenEffect`, where NeoForge keeps the block state in a `Pair` and stores no `BlockState` ("Critical injection failure: Variable modifier method renderScreenEffect(...)"; APPLY-1 recorded it for the next launch). Pre-flight checked only that such a method stores some local. On Forge the handler is Balm 7.3.44's `FabricCropBlockMixin.getGrowthSpeedCaptureLocals` (read in its source): it modifies the `BlockState` at the first call of `BlockState.is(Block)`, vanilla's farmland check inside the loop; Forge's build asks `canSustainPlant` there, so the first call is after the loop, where the state's local is gone, though the method has one (the method-wide check passed it). | No test had a variable modifier picking its local by its type alone where the method has no such local, or none in scope at its point; pre-flight checked only that such a method stores some local. | Fix: a variable modifier picking its local by its type alone (no ordinal, index, name or `argsOnly`), whose methods have no local of that type by their local variable tables, arguments and stores, is treated as Mixin will treat it: where Mixin would stop on it, it is left out (or its mod disabled) with the reason; where Mixin would inject nothing, pre-flight warns. `MixinPreflightTest.aVariableModifierByTypeFailsWhereTheMethodHasNoLocalOfThatType` fails without it. Verified on NeoForge in extended run 41536167292 (on `32c8c5d`): the 1.21.1 corpus client stayed in its world and exited normally, where it had crashed on Puzzles Lib's hook. Forge's: pre-flight also judges the points a call or field `@At` picks, in methods with a local variable table (as Forge's patched methods have): no argument or table entry of the type there, and each slot that held one stored to again since, every instruction from that store to the point covered by the table, so that Mixin's locals there (`Locals.getLocalsAt`) can't have one (`MixinPreflightTest.aVariableModifierByTypeFailsWhereTheLocalIsOutOfScopeAtItsPoint`). Verified on Forge 47 in extended run 41569833541 (on `8cb2da3`): Balm's hook was left out, with the hook keeping state with it (STATE-1), and the corpus server went past the crop hook to CORPUS-68. The mixin declares that method only to call the superclass's, so that its `@Inject` at the method's return has a method to inject into; vanilla's chest has none, and Forge 47 patches the chest to override it. Mixin (0.8.5 and 0.8.7) merges an `@Intrinsic` into a method its target has only for an interface method it renamed, and throws on any other that isn't synthetic, whatever the config. | Pre-flight passed every `@Intrinsic` as one Mixin merges, and the corpus stopped earlier (CORPUS-68). | Fix: an `@Intrinsic` (not displacing) of a class mixin that only calls its superclass's method with its own arguments is dropped where every target declares that method, not static nor abstract: the target's method is kept, as `@Intrinsic` keeps a method its target has, and the injector injects into it; nothing is lost, as the dropped method only stood in for the superclass's (an INFO line in the report). Another `@Intrinsic` the target declares (code of its own, or displacing the target's) is reported as Mixin rejecting the mixin, which disables its mod or leaves the mixin out where it can be (`MixinPreflightTest.anIntrinsicOnlyCallingSuperIsDroppedWhereTheTargetDeclaresIt`). Then no test had an enum Forge makes extensible, and the generated mixin ran only merged by hand, without Forge's extender; and the test's enum had no map by name. | Fix: there, an enum mixin becomes an ordinary mixin adding its constants into the enum's class initializer, as ENUM-5's do (Friends and Foes reads its categories with `valueOf`); one that can't (a constant with a body, code using the mixin, the mod's classes reading a constant as a field of the enum) disables its mod with the reason, as Mixin would stop the game whatever the config (`PipelineTest.anEnumMixinBecomesAMixinAddingItsConstantsWhereMixinAppliesNoEnumMixins`, `EnumValuesMixinsTest.theEnumsValuesArrayIsFoundByItsTypeWhereTheGameRenamedIt`). After the sync (extended run 41569829683) the Forge 66 corpus client and server got past Mixin and stopped in Forge's own enum extender as `MobCategory` loaded ("Enum has create method but we could not find $VALUES", `RuntimeEnumExtender`): `MobCategory` is one of the enums Forge makes extensible (`IExtensibleEnum`), whose extender runs after Mixin and finds the values array only while it is still final, and the generated mixin's `@Mutable` shadow of it had made it non-final. Fix: into such an enum the generated mixin makes the constants with the enum's own `create` factories, which the extender writes, and shadows no values array; a constant whose constructor has no factory leaves it an enum mixin, which pre-flight judges (`EnumValuesMixinsTest.anEnumForgeMakesExtensibleGetsItsConstantsFromItsOwnFactories`, which runs it against a factory written as the extender writes one). After the next sync (extended run 41574438102) every Forge 61 to 66 corpus server and client stopped as `MobCategory` loaded, with a `NullPointerException` in `MobCategory.init` ("MobCategory.BY_NAME is null"): each factory registers its constant in the enum's map by name (Forge's `IExtensibleEnum.init`), which the class initializer sets after the values array, and the generated injector ran right after the array. Fix: into an enum Forge makes extensible, the injector calls the factories at the class initializer's end (`TAIL`), as a Forge mod's own call comes after it, with the map set (the test's enum now registers each constant in a map by name, as `MobCategory`'s factory does). | CORPUS-76 | P0 on Forge 47 clients with REI, ShulkerBoxTooltip or Traveler's Backpack (the corpus client); on Forge 47 for any interface mixin that extends its target for injectors alone | `ModTranslator.leaveOutHandlers`, `AccessorShapedMixins` (new) | The corpus client crashed in its loading overlay (extended run 41590139262, again in 41632355968 and 41648278525): `ClassCircularityError: net/minecraft/client/gui/screens/inventory/tooltip/ClientTooltipComponent` as `ModelBakery.init` loaded the interface, and the three mods' entrypoints threw the same. The stack (two definitions in progress, the second asking for `ClientTooltipComponent` again) means the interface was given a supertype that extends it. The cause, read in Mixin's sources (0.8.5 to Fabric's) against the corpus's mixins (Mixin's export found nothing, as the definition failed first): AppleSkin's `TooltipComponentMixin` is an interface mixin of `ClientTooltipComponent` that extends it, for two static injectors. Forge 47's Mixin 0.8.5 applies no injectors in interface mixins, so pre-flight leaves them out (INTERFACE-1), and the mixin was left with no method. Mixin takes an interface mixin whose methods are all accessors and invokers (synthetic ones aside) for an accessor mixin (`MixinInfo.getVariant`), and makes its targets implement the mixin itself, where an ordinary interface mixin gives them its interfaces less the target: `ClientTooltipComponent` was made to implement an interface extending it. Fabric API's `TooltipComponentMixin` became an accessor mixin the same way, harmlessly, as it extends nothing. | No test left out every method of an interface mixin. | Fixed in core: an interface mixin that leaving out methods turns into an accessor mixin is left out whole where its config lists it and nothing is left for its targets (no accessor, and no interface but its targets); otherwise it no longer names its targets among its interfaces (`AccessorShapedMixins`; `PipelineTest.anInterfaceMixinLeftWithoutItsInjectorsNoLongerMakesItsTargetItsOwnSupertype` fails without it). AppleSkin's and Fabric API's tooltip hooks stay left out on Forge 47 (INTERFACE-1). Verified on Forge 47 after the sync (#259): the corpus client's model loading and REI's, ShulkerBoxTooltip's and Traveler's Backpack's entrypoints no longer throw it (extended run 41658060068). The client now stops later, baking its models (CORPUS-84). |

| CORPUS-74 | P0 on Forge 61 to 66 clients with MoreCulling, and Forge 47's with owo-lib (the corpus clients); on Forge for any mod shadowing a field another mod's mixin adds | `MixinRepairs.sharedFieldRepair` (new), `MixinCompat.shadowsMixinFields` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkMembersOf` | The Forge 26.2 corpus client crashed as Mixin applied MoreCulling's `BlockModelRenderState_fabricCullMixin` (extended run 41590136017): "@Shadow field mesh was not located in the target class net.minecraft.client.renderer.block.BlockModelRenderState", the field Fabric API's renderer mixin adds there. The Forge 47 corpus client crashed the same way on owo's creative screen mixin, which shadows Fabric API's `fabric_currentPage` (extended run 41590139262). Fabric's Mixin finds a `@Shadow` field among the fields of the mixins it prepared before (`TargetClassContext`'s `mixinFields`, from 0.12.0, read in its sources); SpongePowered's, which Forge runs (0.8.5 and 0.8.7), looks in the target's own fields only. Pre-flight took such a shadow as fine wherever an installed mixin adds the field. | No test mod shadowed a field another mixin adds, and the corpus clients stopped earlier. | Fixed in core: where the running Mixin finds only the target's own fields, such a shadow is declared as the other mixin declares the field (its access and signature, Mixin's annotations off), and Mixin, which adds a mixin's new field only where its target has none of that name and descriptor yet, merges the two into one, used by both mods' code as on Fabric; one that can't be declared so (a static shadow of an instance field, a writable one of a final field, an alias) fails pre-flight there (`MixinPreflightTest.aShadowOfAFieldAnotherModsMixinAddsIsThatFieldWhereMixinFindsOnlyTheTargetsOwn`). The Forge test mods count the server's ticks in one mixin's field and read it through another's shadow, which passes on all five Forge versions. Verified in the corpus: MoreCulling 1.8.1 loads on the Forge 65 client, which reaches the world, and Forge 47's client passes owo's shadow (extended runs 41632339327 and 41632355968). |

| CORPUS-75 | P0 on Forge 64 and 65 with Waystones (the corpus servers) | `NamespacedWrapperAliasMixin` (new, both Forge branches) | The 26.1.2 and 26.2 corpus servers stopped loading data packs (extended run 41590136017): "Unknown registry key in ResourceKey[minecraft:root / minecraft:block]: waystones:mossy_waystone" in Waystones' world generation, which names its renamed waystones by their old names. Waystones keeps them as aliases with Fabric API's `FabricRegistry.addAlias`, which Fabric API implements in `MappedRegistry` and resolves where its lookups begin; Forge's `NamespacedWrapper` answers those lookups itself, from its holders and Forge's registry, so the aliases were kept where nothing looked. | No test mod added an alias. | Forge's registries take `addAlias` with Fabric API's checks and its invariant (an alias names an entry, never another alias), add the alias to Forge's registry too (whose own lookups, snapshots and sync to clients then know it), and resolve it where the wrapper's lookups by name and key begin (#254, #249). The test mod registers an item under a new name with its old one as an alias, and the server run with Fabric API checks both lookups and a tag naming it by its old name (all five; PR runs 41592391853 and 41592399896). Verified in the corpus: the Forge 64 and 65 corpus servers load Waystones' data pack and start (extended run 41594474962, #254's c5ced02). |

| NET-10 | P1 for every Cardinal Components API mod on NeoForge (component sync: Trinkets on the 1.21.1 corpus client) | `MeasuredPackets`, `GenericPacketSplitterMeasureMixin`, `PacketEncoderMeasuredMixin` (new; NeoForge's `GenericPacketSplitter`) | The 1.21.1 corpus client logged "Failed to process a synchronized task of the payload: cardinal-components:entity_sync" 19 times (extended run 41512581949): Trinkets' `applySyncPacket` read past the end of an empty buffer (`EOFException` in `FriendlyByteBuf.readNbt`). NeoForge's packet splitter encodes each outgoing packet to measure it, then hands the packet itself to vanilla's encoder, which encodes it again (read in NeoForge's 21.1 and 26.3 sources; singleplayer's connection has the splitter too). Cardinal Components API writes a sync payload's buffer with `writeBytes(ByteBuf)` (`MorePacketCodecs.REG_BYTE_BUF`, read in its 6.1.3 and current sources), which reads the buffer to its end, so the second encoding sent an empty one. Fabric encodes a packet once. Every such mod's component sync failed so on NeoForge, in singleplayer and on a server with NeoForge clients. | No test payload's codec emptied what it wrote: the test mods' payloads wrote plain fields, which a second encoding writes the same. | `01c9258`: for a packet carrying a Fabric payload, the splitter keeps the bytes it measured and vanilla's encoder writes them in place of a second encoding, so the payload's codec runs once, as on Fabric; other packets are coded as NeoForge codes them. The test mods send a payload with Cardinal Components API's codec shape (`NetworkingTest.BufferPing`), which every client checks arrives whole ("payload encoded once"). With Forgified Fabric API providing networking the fix stands down with Fabricated's module, and the check reports what arrived as skipped. Verified in extended run 41527263549 (on `7cc24be`): the check passed in three client runs on each of the five versions, and the 1.21.1 corpus client logged no entity sync failure. |

Scanned 0 target(s)"); the 1.21.1 corpus server crashed as it ticked, on Friends & Foes' `@Inject` at a `PatrollingMonster`'s `finalizeSpawn` in `PatrolSpawner.spawnPatrolMember` (the same failure; extended run 41496005222, which recorded both, APPLY-2). NeoForge's coremods change game classes as they load, before Mixin applies any mod's mixins: one points every `finalizeSpawn` call in the classes it lists (22 on 21.1, 26 from 21.11 on) at `EventHooks.finalizeMobSpawn(Mob, ...)`, which fires `FinalizeSpawnEvent` and makes the call; another reads a few fields (`Biome`'s climate and effects, `Structure`'s settings, `FlowerPotBlock`'s potted block) through NeoForge's getters. The next launch leaves that mixin out and its report says why, until the jar or a build changes; the launch where it first fails still stops (Mixin has half applied the class). Only Mixin's own throwables are believed, and the file is bounded (64 entries, 256 KiB), validated, written atomically and never trusted to name another jar. `MixinFailuresTest`, `MixinFailureRecorderTest`. A gate runs a dedicated server through three launches with the impossible-mixin test mod's pre-flight ignored (one failure through the handlers, one critical failure, then a start without both): every server in pull request run 41438550872. LOADER-2's loader for the jars mods add to the class path replaced that fallback and went to the game loader's parent instead, which has no Gson. Since LOADER-2 (v0.4.0), on NeoForge 21.1 only: FML 10 to 12 make the fallback the loader's parent (read in their `FMLLoader`). The package guard, which the owner suspected, wasn't involved: it already leaves a Fabric jar's copy of a platform package out for every layer the game reads, unexported packages included, and the pack's report shows no Gson package taken. | No test loaded a class only the fallback serves: a new world has no stats file to read, and OneConfig's read only logged a warning, in the informational Polyfrost run. | The loader goes in front of the fallback each platform names (`Fml.gameFallback`: ModLauncher's boot layer loader on NeoForge 21.1, the loader's parent on FML 10 and later). On Forge 64 the server and client stopped building the game's layer (`ResolutionException`: `dev.yumi.commons.collections` and `yumi_commons_collections` export the same package), and on Forge 47 the client did; on the other versions both copies loaded side by side. | No test mod nested a jar listed both ways. | `ad1e2fc`: a translated mod's Forge mod file hides that list on both Forge platforms, in its union file system's filter, as Fabricated reads the jar itself. The Forge test mod nests a Fabric mod listed by `fabric.mod.json` and by a NeoForge jar-in-jar list, and checks that its class is in Fabricated's module of it, the one module of the game's layer with its package; both Forge server smokes gate it. Without the fix the Forge 66 server fails to build the game's layer (`ResolutionException`: module `fabricated.forge.nested` contains the package module `fabricated_forge_nested` exports); with it Forge 66's server smoke passes its 27 checks and Forge 47's its 34. Runs 40570195894 and 40570200260: no corpus run on any Forge version fails to build the game's layer, and LambDynamicLights loads. |
| DAMAGE-1 | P1 on Forge 61 and 64 with Fabric API (Fabric mods' after-damage event) | `fabric-api-plan.json` (Forge 61, 64): `fabric-entity-events-v1`'s `LivingEntityMixin#afterDamage`, replaced by the in-game mod's `LivingEntityDamageMixin` (`DamageEvents`) | Upstream fires `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DAMAGE` at the end of `LivingEntity.hurtServer` with the damage the entity was dealt, which it reads from vanilla's copy of the damage before blocking. Forge 61's and 64's patches drop that copy (they pass the damage taken twice to the criteria triggers). On Forge 64 upstream captures it by name, so pre-flight left the hook out and the event never ran (CI's Forge 64 runs reported it left out). On Forge 61 upstream captures the second float local, there the damage a shield blocked, so the hook applied without a warning and the event reported that as the damage dealt: 0.0 for an unblocked hit (a local Forge 61 server with Fabric API). Forge 65 and 66 keep the copy. | No test hurt an entity with Fabric API; a hook captured by position can't be checked against the platform's locals, and the Forge 64 hook left out was reported and not followed up. | `1f6d837`: the plans skip the hook on Forge 61 and 64, and a mixin of the in-game mod, whose config only those versions name, fires the event with the damage `applyItemBlocking` is asked about, at the method's end, unless the entity is dead or dying. `hurtServer` is wrapped so that a nested call on the same entity leaves the outer call's values. Build-forge run 40544079471: a pig wearing a helmet reports 2.0 dealt and 1.5 taken on every version's server and client with Fabric API. |
| SLEEP-2 | P1 on Forge 47 with Fabric API (Fabric mods' sleep time) | `fabric-api-plan.json` (Forge 47): `fabric-entity-events-v1`'s `PlayerEntityMixin#redirectDaySleepCheck` and `ServerPlayerEntityMixin#redirectDaySleepCheck`, replaced by the in-game mod's `SleepEvents.register` | Upstream asks `EntitySleepEvents.ALLOW_SLEEP_TIME` by redirecting `Level.isDay()` in `Player.tick`, where a sleeping player wakes in the daytime, and in `ServerPlayer.startSleepInBed`. Fabric API for 1.21.11 and later has no such event; the bed rule, an environment attribute, decides. | No test slept in the daytime; the two hooks were reported left out and not followed up. | `8e41469`: the plan skips the two hooks, and the in-game mod answers Forge's event at the lowest priority, when no Forge mod has and the sleeping position is known, from `ALLOW_SLEEP_TIME` with vanilla's answer. | SUPER-1 | P0 on Forge 64 to 66 clients with Fabric API | `DetachedSupers`, `MixinCompat.rebindsInterfaceSuperCalls` (core; applied where SpongePowered's Mixin runs: Forge 61 to 66 and Forge 47) | Past NET-8, a Forge 64 to 66 client with the official Fabric API jar crashed as it drew the first block: "Method 'void SimpleModelWrapper.emitQuads(QuadEmitter, Predicate)' must be Methodref constant" (run 40431467223). Fabric API's renderer gives `SimpleModelWrapper`, a record, a mixin extending `Object` that calls `BlockStateModelPart.super.emitQuads`. Mixin calls such a mixin's super "detached" and rebinds each of its `super` calls to the class in the target's hierarchy that implements the method; for an interface's default, found through the target's own interfaces (Fabricated adds Fabric's injected interfaces to the classes themselves), that is the target, and the call keeps its interface reference, which the JVM refuses. SpongePowered's 0.8.7 does this (`MixinTargetContext.updateStaticBinding`, read in its source); Fabric's Mixin stopped rebinding calls to interfaces in 0.15.0 (FabricMC/Mixin#150), so NeoForge, whose renderer module is native anyway, is unaffected. | No Forge client ran with the Fabric API jar. | `cd3e633`: where the running Mixin is one of SpongePowered's releases, a kept mixin whose superclass isn't its targets' one and that calls an interface's method as its super gets its targets' superclass (in its declaration, generic signature and constructors' super call, found as Mixin finds it), so Mixin keeps its calls as they are; a mixin that also calls its own superclass's methods as its super keeps it. `DetachedSupersTest` covers it. In runs 40433805134 and 40433807263 it changes Fabric API's `SimpleModelWrapperMixin` on Forge 64 to 66 and nothing on Forge 61 or 47, and the client with the Fabric API jar reaches a world on all five, every check passing. |

| ORDER-1 | P0 | `Pipeline.loadAfter`, `ModsTomlWriter` (every platform) | Each translated mod's descriptor ordered it `AFTER` every present Fabric mod it depends on or recommends, and nothing checked for loops. Fabric lets mods depend on each other, in pairs (A and B) or rings (A, B, C), and orders nothing by dependencies, while NeoForge's and Forge's sorters stop the game at any cycle of orderings: "Mod Sorting failed", "Detected Cycles" (reported on Forge 47; the same `CyclePresentException` handling is in NeoForge's FML and in Forge 61 to 66's, read in their sources). | No test or CI run had two mods depending on each other: each mod's orderings were built and tested alone. | `LoadOrderings.withoutCycles` walks the Fabric mods' orderings depth first, in id order, and leaves out each ordering back to a mod still on its path: only orderings in a cycle go, a pair or a ring loses one, and none is left (the walk keeps its own stack, as the jars are untrusted). The report names each one under *Load order*. Orderings outside a cycle, and those after NeoForge or Forge mods, are unchanged; the cache key already holds the orderings, so cached overlays are rebuilt. `LoadOrderingsTest` (pairs, rings, linked rings, a diamond, a tail into a cycle whichever way its id sorts, a 200,000-mod chain) and `PipelineTest` (a pair, a ring and a dependent, on NeoForge's and Forge's descriptors: no cycle, everything else kept) pass, and each platform's server smoke now loads two Fabric mods that depend on each other and must start. Not covered: a cycle through a NeoForge or Forge mod's own orderings, which core can't see; no case seen. |

Otherwise the injector stays and is reported. `MixinPreflightTest.injectorsOnAMethodNeoForgeReducedToACallWithAnInsertedParameterMoveThroughBridges` (the bridges pass the JVM's verifier). |

A constant with a body overriding the one method the enum forwards to a delegate (`GrassColorModifier`'s `ColorModifier`) is made with the constructor taking the delegate and a class generated next to the mod's mixins that runs the body. Only straight-line code using nothing of the mixin moves, and nothing when a class of the mod reads a constant as a field of the enum, which the mixin can't add; the mod is disabled then, as before. `EnumValuesMixinsTest` builds such an enum and mixin, merges them as Mixin would, and runs the class initializer. | PRE-7 | P2 | `MixinPreflight` (ShulkerBoxTooltip, every version) | ShulkerBoxTooltip's tooltip mixin was disabled: its `@WrapOperation` in `AbstractContainerScreen.extractTooltip` wraps a call to `GuiGraphicsExtractor.setTooltipForNextFrame(Font, List, Optional, int, int, Identifier)` (with a trailing `boolean` on some versions), and NeoForge's build of the method calls an overload that also takes an `ItemStack`. | Pre-flight repaired a call made through a subtype (PRE-1), not one with another argument. | This pass: an injector at a call the game build makes only through an overload with one more argument, whose position its type tells apart, is pointed at that overload. Handlers that see the call's arguments (`@Redirect`, `@WrapWithCondition`, `@WrapOperation`) get a bridge taking the overload's, which calls them without the added argument; a `@WrapOperation`'s `Operation` puts it back for the original call. The handler stays a private method. An injector in a named `@Group` is left alone, by this repair and every other that points an injector elsewhere: it is one of the mod's alternatives, and moving it made two of Iris's `@Group(max = 1)` pair apply on 26.2 (CI run 351, Iris's Fabric build). A bridge takes the call's exact types, so a handler whose receiver is a `@Coerce`d supertype isn't bridged (the bridge wouldn't carry the annotation, and Mixin would refuse it); generated methods get names the mixin doesn't use yet, as handlers may share a name; and the former handler is `@Unique`, so Mixin renames it rather than merging it over another mixin's method of the same name (all three found by this pass's review). Unit tests, one running a bridge; `TRANSLATION_SCHEMA` 22. CI (run 351): ShulkerBoxTooltip loads on all four versions. |

Found with PRE-7's regression. | This pass: such an injector requires nothing. Group minimums and maximums are not checked (Mixin checks them at apply time). Unit test. |

| LIB-1 | P1 | `LibraryBridges`, `ModTranslator.withLibraryBridges` (Carpet, any mod giving a library class its interfaces) | Carpet's mixins into Brigadier's `CommandNode` and `CommandDispatcher` only give them its interfaces `CommandNodeInterface` and `CommandDispatcherInterface`, implemented with their private maps, to remove a script's commands (read in its `CommandNode_scarpetCommandsMixin`, `CommandDispatcher_scarpetCommandsMixin` and `script.external.Vanilla`). NeoForge loads Brigadier on the class path, without mixin support, so pre-flight disabled Carpet on every version (advanced corpus, run on `bc2bc1b`): the mixins would never apply, and Carpet's cast would fail. | Pre-flight could only fail such a mixin. | This pass: such a mixin (a class with one target in a library, only `@Shadow` fields, a trivial constructor, no injector or other Mixin feature, and interfaces of the mod that it alone implements, with no default methods) is left out of its config and replaced by a generated class next to its first interface. The mixin's methods become static methods of that class, taking the library object first; its shadowed fields are read and written through `java.lang.reflect.Field`, which works as Brigadier is in the unnamed module (its stack frames carry no module name in the runs' logs). The mod's casts to the interfaces, `instanceof` checks and calls of their methods, method references included, call the class. Any other mixin into a library class still fails pre-flight. `LibraryBridgesTest` compiles a library, mixins and a mod as Carpet's with javac, translates them, loads the result and runs it; `PipelineTest.aModWhoseMixinOnlyGivesALibraryClassAnInterfaceLoadsWithABridge`. `TRANSLATION_SCHEMA` 34. |

The mixin stays in its config: Mixin loads an accessor mixin as an ordinary interface (its passthrough of loadable mixins), finds the library class through the context class loader (FML's `FMLClassBytecodeProvider`, read in its source) and never applies it there. The bridge goes outside the mixin package, whose other classes Mixin refuses to load. `LibraryBridgesTest.anAccessorMixinIntoALibraryClassBecomesABridgeTheModCalls`, `PipelineTest.aModWithAnAccessorMixinIntoALibraryClassLoadsWithABridge`. Invokers and static accessors are still judged by pre-flight. |
| NET-6 | P1 | `PacketDecoderContextMixin`, `PacketEncoderContextMixin` (Polymer on 26.x, any codec reading Fabric's packet context) | Fabric API's 26.x networking binds the connection's `PacketContext` (a scoped value) while each packet is encoded and decoded, so a codec can read it through `PacketContext.get()` (upstream's `PacketDecoderMixin`, `PacketEncoderMixin`). Fabricated gave connections a context but never bound it, and Polymer's hello payload failed to decode on the 26.2 advanced client ("Cannot invoke PacketContext.get(ReadKey) because context is null", run on `c725f6f`), which closed the connection. | The test mods' codecs read no context. | This pass: decoding and encoding run with the connection's context bound, the connection being the channel pipeline's handler of its type. |
| NET-7 | P1 | `GenericPacketSplitterContextMixin`, `ServerLoginPacketListenerImplMixin`, `client.ClientHandshakePacketContextMixin`, `client.ClientPacketListenerContextMixin` (Polymer on 26.x, any codec reading Fabric's packet context) | With NET-6, the 26.2 and 26.3 advanced clients joined their worlds, then lost the connection (run on `9b2e532`). NeoForge's packet splitter encodes each packet before vanilla's encoder to measure it, and decodes the packets it reassembles; neither ran with the context bound, so Polymer's ingredient codec failed on Fabric's recipe sync ("PacketContext.get() is null"). Fabricated also never put into the context what Fabric API puts there (upstream's login, handshake and configuration listener mixins): the server and its registries, the player's profile, the client's registries. Polymer warned that chunk packets lacked the registries, and its item codecs fell back to a lookup of their own. | The test mod sends a 3 MiB payload whose codec checks the context while the server encodes it and the client decodes it, which the client smoke test checks on 26.x. | This pass: the splitter codes with the connection's context bound, and the context holds the server and its registries from the login listener's creation, the profile from login, and the client's registries from the play listener's creation. Also: `PacketContext`'s constants were `PacketContextImpl`'s keys, and initializing a class first initializes the interfaces with default methods it implements. When the impl class initialized first (a connection's first context), it initialized `PacketContext` before its own keys were set, so its constants stayed null: every lookup through `PacketContext.REGISTRY_ACCESS` and the others found nothing, `CONNECTION` included. The runs where a mod touched `PacketContext` first passed; the test mods alone and the recipe viewers didn't (CI on `27caea2` and `38c4ce3`: "registries false, profile false, server false, connection null"; making the keys without `PacketContext.key`, on `1c3710c`, didn't change that, and a standalone class pair shows the rule). The keys now live in `PacketContextKeys`, which both read, so either order works. |

That stopped the 1.21.11 advanced corpus client (run on `bc2bc1b`). | Fabricated only handled Fabric payload types, which are all registered with NeoForge. | This pass: `checkPacket` lets through, both ways, a payload whose id NeoForge has no registration for in any protocol, as vanilla sends any payload; a registered one without a channel is still refused, and a Fabric one is still dropped first (`ServerPayloadSendMixin`). The streamed caps (128 MiB an entry, 512 MiB an archive) held. | The tests' bombs declared their real sizes. | Health pass after PR #64: an entry holding more bytes than its header declares is refused when read. `SafeArchiveTest.refusesAnEntryHoldingMoreThanItsHeaderDeclares`. |

Reproduced with a stand-in NeoForge mod of that id and package on 1.21.1 and 1.21.11. | CI never ran Fabricated next to a NeoForge mod carrying Fabric API's packages. | The owner's policy, "yield to theirs": a Fabric API module another mod provides (by its id, its `provides`, or one of the module's packages in its jar) is that mod's. The first stand-in run found a second fault: Fabric API keeps `net.fabricmc.fabric.api.util` in two modules, and the index gave it to `fabric-content-registries-v0`, so a mod with `fabric-api-base` was taken for providing content registries too; the index script now refuses a shared package without an owner, and it is `fabric-api-base`'s, as in Forgified Fabric API. Verified: `NativeModulesTest`, `PipelineTest.aNativeModuleAnotherModProvidesIsThatModsOnly`; server runs with `ci/neoforge/standins.py`'s mods on 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2, every check passing (the test mods' among them, on the provider's copies). On Forge 47 and 61 to 66 (task 3, #240 and #241): the same policy, from core's platform-neutral parts, for Forge's native modules (all six of Forge 47's are ones Forgified Fabric API for 1.20.1 provides), their mixins and their bridges; a server run per Forge version with stand-ins is a gate. |

On NeoForge 21.1 the game's layer reads both and resolution fails, with or without Fabric mods (reproduced with a stand-in). On FML 10 and later plugin libraries load with a plain class loader, so nothing fails to resolve, but the copy's own classes and its language loader service still ran. | As PROVIDER-1. | The owner's policy, "always ours": an FML mod file reader ahead of FML's own reads any jar but a Fabricated build without `net/fabricmc/loader/`, `net/fabricmc/api/` and the service files naming their classes: filtered contents on FML 4, a copy without them in `.fabricated/loader-api/` on FML 10 and later (which can't filter a plugin library), of the same type. A first version made such a jar a game library on FML 10 and later; with a stale Forge build of Fabricated beside the NeoForge one (a local run), that put its copies of Fabricated's classes in the game's layer, ahead of the real ones, and the 1.21.11 stand-in run stopped. Every Fabricated build is now left alone and the type kept. Verified: the stand-in runs of PROVIDER-1, whose nested Fabric Loader copy has a language loader service naming one of its classes. On Forge (task 3, #240 and #241, the owner's decision of 2026-11-02): Forge has no reader that can leave another jar's copy out, and beside Fabricated's, Forge 47 stopped building the game's layer with "ResolutionException: Modules net.fabricmc.loader and io.github.cmdralph.fabricated export package net.fabricmc.loader.launch.common", naming neither mod (the diagnostic run of Forgified Fabric API for 1.20.1, 41371266132). The mod locator refuses a Forge jar with its own copy, or nesting one (`FabricLoaderCopies`, bounded in depth and entries), at once, naming the jar and the nested one (`ConflictingMods$LaunchRefused`, which Forge's discovery doesn't collect, so Forge doesn't go on to an error screen that would fail the same way). A refusal server per Forge version with a stand-in nesting Fabricated's own classes is a gate. Coexisting is task 5. |

deprecated 4-argument `SectionCompiler.compile`. It now targets the 5-argument overload by descriptor (`0dbdfda`,
